日本：旺盛なAI関連需要が製造業の景況感を押し上げ。利益計画は大幅上方修正
3
 大企業の業況判断は製造業が6四半期連続の改善。非製造業は悪化も一時的な悪天候が主因で変調の懸念は低い
― 業種別では、化学、石油・石炭製品、生産用機械等が改善。旺盛なAI関連需要や原油の供給不足懸念の後退等が背景

― 一方、非製造業は、長雨を受けて小売や対個人サービス、宿泊・飲食サービスが悪化
 2026年度の経常利益計画は、製造業中心に大幅上方修正。9月調査時点としては過去5年間で最高の増益計画
― とりわけ大企業・製造業では、輸出向け売上高の上方修正が顕著。上期の実績が大幅に上振れたことを反映したもの
― 世界的なAI関連需要の好調持続が見込まれるもと、下期も堅調な輸出が続けば、利益計画は一段の上方修正余地

4
 2026年度の設備投資計画は前年度比＋9.3％と高い伸びを維持
― 製造業は6月調査からほぼ横ばいの同＋7.1％。非製造業は同＋11.8％と1.1％Ptの上方修正
 AI需要による好業績を踏まえれば製造業の投資計画にはやや慎重さがにじむ内容
― 一方、生産・営業用設備判断DIは2025年度後半以降、設備不足方向への動きが鮮明。高水準の内部資金も踏まえれ
ば、増産投資などを中心に今後の設備投資が上振れる余地
― なお、今後の利上げが企業の投資姿勢に及ぼす影響には留意
25年度後半以降、急速
な設備不足超を示唆

日本：企業の価格設定姿勢はなお強い。仕入価格再上昇にも警戒
5
 大企業・製造業の販売価格判断DIは前期並みの高水準を維持。企業は積極的な価格転嫁姿勢を継続
―6・7月の油価下落等を背景に素材業種の仕入価格判断DIが低下。対して販売価格判断DIは横ばい。コスト低下局面で
も販売価格を維持しており、企業利益の押し上げ要因に
 企業の中長期的な物価見通しも2％台半ばと、日銀の物価目標を上回る水準で高止まり
― 足元の原油価格再上昇やタンカー運賃高騰を受け、仕入価格が再び上昇する可能性には留意。企業の価格設定姿勢
が一段と強まるリスクも

日本：利上げでも企業金融は安定。企業部門の金利耐性を確認
6
 資金繰り判断は総じて底堅い。これまでの利上げの影響は限定的
― 利上げの影響が懸念された中小企業の資金繰り判断は横ばい圏の推移。価格転嫁の進展などを受けた事業環境の改
善が資金繰りを支えている構図
― 金利上昇局面でも銀行貸出は増加基調を維持しており、企業の資金需要も底堅く推移
 企業収益や資金繰りが金利上昇への耐性を示す中、金融環境はなお緩和的
― 市場のターミナルレート予想は2.5％程度まで上昇し、日銀の中立金利推計レンジ上限に接近

（%）
欧州：G7は原油・ディーゼルの協調放出で合意。米国の輸出制限リスクは後退
7
 G7はIEAを通じ4カ月で1億バレルの石油備蓄放出に合意。初期20日間はディーゼルを前倒し放出
― 背景にディーゼル価格高騰。中間選挙を控えたトランプ大統領は、禁輸も辞さない姿勢を強調（米国は世界需要の4.9％
を供給）。米国からの輸出が欧州の需要を支える一方、米国よりも潤沢な在庫を保有する欧州に圧力をかけた格好
― 欧州は石油製品消費に占めるディーゼル比率が高く、価格高騰を回避する思惑も協調放出の合意を後押し
― 合意ではG7域内でのエネルギー輸出制限回避も確認され、米国によるEU向け禁輸リスクはひとまず後退
石油製品の製品別消費割合の国際比較
（出所）Energy Institute より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 9

欧州：ディーゼル需給逼迫は残存。欧州のインフレ上振れ要因に
8
 ロシアのディーゼル輸出は足元で急減。中東からの輸出減、中国の輸出停止も重なり、世界需要の約6％が失われた格好
―G7の1億バレル放出で当面の供給不安は緩和するものの、構造的な供給不足の解消には至らず
―IEAも、中東からの原油輸出は回復する一方、石油製品の供給制約はなお深刻と指摘。ディーゼル需給の逼迫が続け
ば、輸送・物流コストを通じたインフレ圧力が残存
 欧州では9月HICPが再加速。ディーゼル高が輸送・物流コストを通じて追加的なインフレ上振れ要因となるリスク
―9月消費者物価（HICP）は前年比＋3.8％（前月：同＋3.2％）と伸び加速。エネルギー価格の上昇が主因
今般の備蓄放出量と停止中のディーゼル輸出
（注） 世界需要およびロシア・中国の輸出量は2025年実績。中東は2026年8月実績を参
考に、輸出量を前年の4分の1としてシェアを概算
（出所）EIA、IEA、OPEC、各種報道より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

注目点②G7備蓄放出でもくすぶるディーゼル供給不安

米国：雇用は減速も、AI関連需要が下支え
 9月の非農業部門雇用者数は前月差＋2.9万人と市場予想（同＋9.0万人）を下回る結果。7月・8月も下方修正
― 民間部門が前月差＋4.6万人（8月同+8.9万人）と増勢が鈍化したほか、政府部門が同▲1.7万人と再びマイナス転化
― 民間部門では、旺盛なAI投資需要などを背景に、製造や建設、建設・エンジニアリングなどが増加基調を維持したもの
の、情報、金融、専門サービスなどの高賃金セクターが減少
― 時間当たり賃金の伸びは前月比＋0.1％（8月同＋0.3％）と鈍化。こちらも市場予想（同＋0.3％）を下回る結果
― 総じて冴えない結果ながら、3カ月平均では＋5万人程度を維持しており、雇用は減速しつつも失速には至らず

米国：対人型業種の雇用に底堅さ。労働参加率上昇で失業率は小幅上昇
 対人接触度の高い業種では雇用に底堅さ、AIによる代替が進みにくい可能性
― ヘルスケアや建設、飲食サービスなど、対人接触機会の多い業種では雇用が増加する一方、経理・会計サービス、ITシ
ステム開発などでは低調
 失業率は4.2％と小幅悪化も引き続き低水準を維持
― 主に16-24歳の若年層およびプライムエイジ層の労働参加率の上昇が押し上げに寄与した模様

中国：景況感は急速に改善。建設業が持ち直し。政府は住宅ローンの利子補給で市場テコ入れ
 9月の総合PMIは50.7と、前月（49.5）から大幅に上昇。景況感が急速に改善。建設業が大きく持ち直し
― 製造業PMIが50.1（前月：49.8）、非製造業PMIが50.2（前月：49.0）とともに節目回復。製造業の生産（51.7）が堅調
― 建設業PMIは50.3と、前月（46.9）から急上昇。異常気象の影響はく落と財政予算執行の本格化があるとみられる
 中国政府が9/28、新規住宅ローン金利の年率1％分を補助する政策を発表。低迷が続く不動産市場をテコ入れ
― 対象物件の購入金額を150万元（3,500万円）以下に限定し、低中所得者層の住宅ローン負担を軽減する
― これにより、余剰在庫に苦しむ地方都市での実需を掘り起こし、市況のさらなる悪化に歯止めをかけたい考え

### Page 13

 中国のASEAN向け輸出品は、ASEANの製造品と競合。競合分野は一般機械や玩具、食品など幅広に
― 一般機械等に加え、ベトナムは電子機器や紙・木・繊維等の軽工業品、インドネシアは輸送機械でも競合度が高い
 中国製品流入が強まる中、ASEANはアンチダンピング、セーフガード措置に加え、越境ECへの規制強化などで対応
― 素材・中間財についてはアンチダンピング措置で対応。最終財では、低価格輸入品への課税や価格規制で応じる方向
― 中国企業等による規制回避を懸念し、ASEAN側が対象品目拡張や反迂回措置を強化する動きもあるが、こうした措置
の実効性は不透明
アジア：中国製品との競争激化でASEANの防衛策が拡大

 2026年以降に相違が目立つ台湾と韓国におけるAI需要の恩恵

日本：コアCPIは政策補助効果のはく落を主因に加速。その背後で徐々に高まるインフレ圧力
14
 9月の都区部コアCPIは前年比＋2.7％（前月：同＋1.8％）と大幅加速。政策要因のはく落に加え、価格転嫁の影響も拡大
― 東京都で実施された水道基本料金無償化・保育所保育料無償化の影響がはく落し、全体の伸びを＋0.56％Pt押し上げ

― 食品ではコメ価格の下落が続いた一方、コメを除いた食品価格は前月から伸び加速。

上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 聯準會上周三決議升息一碼至 3.75%-4.00%區間，FOMC 政策聲明顯示，18 名
FOMC 成員中有 12 人預計今年將再升息一次 ， 另有4 人認為存在升息兩次的可
能性 ，加上聯準會主席華許(Kevin Warsh)會後表達控制通膨的偏鷹立場 ， 帶動美
元指數走升至 100 高位。
⚫ 歐元區 9 月 ZEW 經濟景氣指數自前月的 31.4 降至 25.8，低於預估的 39.9。數
據下滑顯示在美伊衝突未獲緩解 、 能源價格持續高位情況下 ， 市場對於歐元區經
濟前景抱持較不樂觀的態度。
⚫ 美國公布初請失業金人數為 19.6 萬人，優於市場預期的 20.8 萬，再度呼應聯準
會主席華許(Kevin Warsh)認為目前勞動力市場仍具韌性的說法， 在美國經濟穩
健及通膨頑固的情況下，預計聯準會將持續升息，有利美元持穩。
⚫ 歐洲央行管委暨葡萄牙央行總裁佩雷拉(Alvaro Santos Pereira)表示，歐元區目前

通膨雖未廣泛擴散 ， 惟考量近期天然氣價格飆升 ， 央行將密切關注未來通膨壓力
擴散情形。最新利率期貨市場顯示，歐洲央行今年底仍有再升息一碼空間。
⚫ 日本政府通過消費稅減稅及家庭現金補貼的政策大綱，預計自明年 4 月起的兩
年內，將食品消費稅率自 8%降至 1%，惟大綱中未明確說明資金來源，再度引
發市場對於日本政府的財政擔憂，短線不利日圓表現。
⚫ 台灣央行 9/17 宣布重貼現率連 10 凍維持在 2%不變，央行總裁楊金龍表示，台
灣的通膨相較其他主要經濟體溫和，採取漸進緊縮貨幣政策有助物價穩定。 此
外，目前通膨率為 2%，明年有望降至 2%以下，後續將觀察通膨發展、主要經
濟體貨幣政策緊縮程度及台灣金融情勢，以決定未來貨幣政策走向。

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.650，低點在 31.958，以 31.808 作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 1 角 7 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶 ， 美國CPI 數據公布後加強市場對於聯準會 9 月升息的預期 ，
早盤台幣跳空開低，外資連續三日賣超台股並匯出，加以出口商態度轉為惜售，令
台幣接連貶破 31.700、31.800、31.900 關卡， 週四由於聯準會偏鷹派升息，美元指
數走升至 100.300，早盤台幣跳空開低，台幣最低貶至31.958，不過隨後出口商賣壓
出籠，外資雙向進出，台幣收復部分跌幅，維持在31.900 附近震盪；臨近周末，外
資買超台股並於外匯市場匯入 ， 台幣走升至31.722， 最終在央行調節之下收在31.808。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣區間震盪 。 就國際市場而言 ， 美國最新初領失業救濟金人數較預
期減少一萬多人、續領失業救濟金人數亦下降，顯示美國勞動力市場尚屬穩健，加
以聯準會升息抗通膨，鷹派立場給予美元指數支撐、不利台幣表現；就台灣市場來
看，九月台灣央行並未升息，台美利差擴大預計將帶給台幣壓力，此外，儘管聯準
會升息利空出盡，外資買超台股帶動台幣走升，惟台灣即將迎來中秋連假，預計金
融市場交投謹慎，將令台幣陷入橫盤整理格局，綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢區間
震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Sep/21/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走升。人民幣中間價連續走升，接連升破 6.7700、6.7600 整數
關口，升至三年七個月來高點，帶動人民幣偏升，不過由於美元指數震盪走強，加
以市場靜待聯準會利率決議公布 ， 交投謹慎 ， 人民幣升幅有限 ， 大致維持高檔震盪 ，
週四聯準會鷹派升息一碼，惟人民幣中間價連續五日走升至 6.7521，並創下四年高
點，帶動人民幣緩步走升，USD/CNH 升破 6.7000 整數大關，終場境外人民幣收在
6.6944，週線升值 94 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6984，週線升值 21 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣震盪走升 。 就國際市場來看 ，FED 最新政策聲明和經濟預測顯
示，聯準會為明年繼續收緊貨幣政策敞開了大門，美元指數走升，抑制人民幣升值
的速度；就中國國內市場來看，中間價持續偏升將帶動人民幣走升，加以出口商實
質拋匯需求將支撐人民幣表現； 最後就技術面來看 ，USD/CNH 日線圖中 ， 匯價維持
在五日均線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，加以USD/CNH 有效跌破 6.7000 整數關
卡，顯示 USD/CNH 空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣震盪走升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6700-6.7200

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週日圓走貶。 週初，美國通膨數據維持高位 ，令聯準會升息預期升溫 ，USD/JPY
自 153.29 低點反彈至 155 附近；週間 ，美國聯準會宣布升息一碼 ， 帶動USD/JPY 上
漲至 156.41；臨近週末，日本央行如市場預期升息一碼，惟央行偏鴿派立場令日圓
續貶，帶動 USD/JPY 一度最高來到 158.05，而隨後在日本政府於外匯市場進行匯率
詢價下，USD/JPY 回吐部分漲幅，終場收在 156.86，週線上漲 349 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本政府上周通過消費稅減
稅及家庭現金補貼政策大綱，惟大綱中未明確說明財源，再度引發市場對於日本政
府財政惡化的擔憂，預計將不利日圓走勢。二、日本央行上周五決議將政策利率調
升一碼至 1.25%， 9 名決策委員中有 2 人對升息持反對意見，降低市場對日本央行
進一步升息的預期。另一方面 ， 聯準會上周三亦升息一碼至3.75%-4.00%區間 ，FOMC
政策聲明顯示，18 位官員中有 12 人預計今年將再升息一次，另有 4 人認為存在升
息兩次的可能性，聯準會升息立場明顯較日本央行更為鷹派，預計將支撐USD/JPY
走勢。三、日本財務大臣片山皋月近期再度重申，將於必要時採取外匯干預行動，
以維護外匯市場有序運行。四、就技術面而言，在日線圖中，USD/JPY 短中天期均
線向下發散走勢偏空，惟近期匯價在多次向下測試 154 均拉回，明顯下方有買盤支
撐。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：154.80-159.50

美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，聯準會升息預期升溫加上歐元區 9 月經濟景氣指
數下滑，令歐元走跌至 1.1525；週間，美國聯準會鷹派升息立場帶動美元走升，歐
元最低來到 1.1460；臨近週末，歐元持續偏弱整理，終場收在 1.1483，週線下跌 107
點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、 歐洲央行管委卡薩克斯
(Martins Kazaks) 表示 ， 為避免美伊戰爭引發的燃料成本上漲開始傳導至工資和其他
商品價格，歐洲央行可能需要漸進式調升利率以抑制通膨。最新利率期貨市場顯示
歐洲央行今年仍有升息一碼的空間，預計將支撐歐元走勢。二、美國聯準會決議將
利率調升一碼至 3.75%-4.00%區間，聯準會主席華許會後偏鷹言論帶動美元指數走
升至 100 上方，預計將限縮歐元走升空間。三、歐元區 9 月 ZEW 經濟景氣指數自
前月的 31.4 降至 25.8，低於預估的 39.9。數據下滑顯示在美伊衝突未獲緩解、能源
價格持續高位情況下，市場對於歐元區經濟前景抱持較不樂觀的態度，將不利歐元
表現。四、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 近期回測年線未能有效突破後明顯
拉回轉為下行走勢，目前匯價承壓於各天期均線下方，空方動能較佔優勢。綜合以
上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。

＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1350-1.1590

                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.09.14-115.09.18 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.70% 
公債－10 年期-A15106 1.92 % ~ 1.921% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.86% ~ 1.861%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ， 本週央行理監事會議決議結果 ， 維持政策利率不變 ， 符合
市場預期 ， 且近兩週央行持續減發定存單 ， 令市場短錢充裕 ， 惟週五銀行間分
配略為不均，部分銀行有資金需求，因此短率持穩於近期區間。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.70%，利率高位較上週上升 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週市場交投清淡，5 年期指標券 A15108 週三等殖系統成交在 1.86%，10 年
期指標券 A15106 則未成交，最近成交利率為 1.92~1.921%。 
台灣央行理監事會議如市場預期維持政策利率不變 ， 總裁楊金龍表示 ， 今年國
內通膨仍屬可控，明年有望回落至 2%以下，市場對今年升息疑慮有所降溫，
對台債形成支撐 ， 惟國際債市走勢仍偏空 ， 預期台債殖利率短線仍呈高檔區間
盤整格局，後續關注下週三財政部公布的第四季公債發行計劃，預估 5 年券
區間為 1.79%~1.83%，10 年券區間為 1.90%~1.95%。

### Page 5

Server TAM estimate revisions

AI server racks: We raise our forecast for AI server racks powered by NVIDIA to 61k / 
97k / 149k in 2026 - 28E (vs. 50k / 92k / 148k previously), reflecting the increase in our 
forecasts for brand makers (Dell, SuperMicro, HP), leading to higher US CSP capex by 4% 
/ 17% / 22% in 2026-28E, and NVIDIA chipset shipments (raised by 9% / 15% in 2027 / 
28E), showing the ongoing upcycle of AI infrastructure. Overall, we expect the AI server 
rack global value TAM to grow at 163% CAGR in 2025-28E to US$1tn, or 63% of the 
global server value TAM by 2028E. By vendor, we continue to expect global leading CSPs 
to be the major customers, with leading ODM direct players as the major suppliers. We 
expect Hon Hai / FII to maintain the leading market position among ODMs and with 
continuous specification upgrade driving its global market share expansion.

AI servers: We raise AI server (8-GPU) equivalent shipment by 10% / 29% in 2027-28E 
to 2.7m / 3.3m in 2027-28E, mainly driven by rising ASIC-based AI server demand. The 
custom-design provides ASIC-based AI servers optimized for AI computation with better 
efficiency, such as lower latency, or memory savings via reduced expensive read/write 
cycles. We continue to see new generation ASICs launched by global leading CSPs, 
supporting AI server growth: we expect the AI servers (8-GPU) global value TAM to grow

### Page 6

at 38% CAGR in 2025-28E to US$318bn, or 20% of global server value TAM by 2028E. 
We reduce 2026E shipment by 7% to 1.7m, reflecting a shift toward higher density AI 
server racks driven by leading US CSPs.

General servers: We raise general server revenue by 15% / 13% / 11% in 2026-28E 
driven by high memory costs and ongoing specification upgrades driving AI workloads. 
Intel launched its Intel Xeon 6+ processors at Computex 2026 (link) to facilitate Agentic 
AI workloads, positioning: CPU servers as the control plane, driving system-level 
performance and efficiency under rising agentic AI workloads, while supporting data 
movement and sustained inferencing. Intel presented Xeon 7 Diamond Rapids in Aug 
2026 (link), with architectures for Agentic AI and Enterprise scale workloads, 
empowering efficient inference and edge computing. AMD has begun production ramp 
of its 6th Gen AMD EPYC CPUs (Venice) in May (link), which powers agentic AI and 
diverse workloads - running the agents, AI host nodes, and general purposes that turns 
intelligence into action with optimized TCO (total cost of ownership). We expect general 
servers’ global value TAM to grow at 22% CAGR in 2025-28E to US$271bn, or 17% of 
global server value TAM by 2028E.

CSP capex: Regarding customer spending, our US internet team forecasts that leading 
US cloud service providers (CSPs) in aggregate will grow their capex budgets by 84%/ 
52%/ 13% YoY in 2026-28E, with our estimates being raised by 4% / 17% / 22% in 
2026-28E to US$1,397bn by 2028E. Within the China internet space, we expect leading

### Page 7

Chinese cloud platforms to grow capex by 105%/ 21%/ 14% YoY in 2026-28E, with our 
estimates being raised by 14% / 15% / 11% in 2026-28E. The strong increase in CSP 
capex reflects the high memory costs and growing demand on AI workloads. In terms of 
capex structure, we estimate chipsets (GPU, ASIC, CPU) remain the major contributors, 
along with memory (HBM), and followed by networking and other components (e.g. 
cooling, power, chassis, rail kits, etc.). Compared to global CSPs, we estimate China CSP 
spending on chipsets would be more diversified across GPU and ASICs considering 
various local fabless suppliers and customers’ varying demand on AI scenarios / tasks. 
Read more: Microsoft, Amazon, Meta, Alphabet, Oracle, Baba, Tencent, Baidu.  
Key highlights on the recent Maia 300 announcement from MSFT: (1) Microsoft to 
expand its Azure Maia system to multi-gigawatt deployments, making Maia the primary 
inference system for Microsoft products and services, and (2) Microsoft plans to have 
multi-gigawatt Maia deployment beginning in 2027, with reports indicating a potential 
plan to deploy >1M Maia 300 chips in 2027 (Microsoft is covered by Gabriela Borges).

Improving 3Q GPM helped by better mix — Dynapack's preliminary 3Q26 results 
showed margin recovery yet weak PC sales remained the key drag on the earnings. 
3Q26 EPS of NT$2.33 (+72% q/q and +49% y/y) missed our/BBG est by 45%/27%, 
although we believe the earnings shortfall was well anticipated given the 3Q sales 
miss owing to the delayed shipment of one of the BBU products and weaker PC 
demand. GPM improved q/q on a richer product mix, with higher-margin BBU 
making up c.50% of 3Q sales on our estimate, while OPM recovered to 10.7%, 
+4.2ppt q/q and +1.3ppt y/y. Nevertheless, the sales shortfall constrained op 
leverage, leaving margin improvement insufficient to offset weaker sales. 
PSU vendor shortages weigh on Oct; 12kW ramp to drive Nov sales rebound — 
Our industry checks suggest that Dynapack's major PSU vendor has encountered 
component supply shortages (related to power management IC) and therefore 
delayed BBU pull-ins, weighing on shipments of Dynapack's existing 3kW and 5kW 
BBU products in Oct. We therefore expect Oct-26 BBU sales to decline slightly m/m. 
Meanwhile, PC sales should recover sequentially and more than to offset the BBU 
softness, supporting a slight m/m increase in group sales. Looking beyond Oct-26, 
we expect accelerating shipments of the new 12kW BBU series from Nov-26 to drive 
a strong rebound in BBU sales.

Implications — We reiterate our bullish stance on the BBU sector despite near-term
volatility from external supply-chain constraints, which is likely to put pressure on
near-term sentiment. We view the component-driven delays in product pull-ins
primarily as a shipment-timing issue, with underlying demand remaining strong into
2027E. We also see ample opportunity for Dynapack to gain market share from

Panasonic, its major Japanese peer, particularly as power architectures migrate
toward HVDC. This share-gain potential remains intact, reinforcing our confidence
in Dynapack's medium term BBU growth outlook.

Dynapack International
Valuation
We assign a 32x target P/E to our 2027E EPS of NT$25 to derive a target price of NT$800. We value Dynapack based on 2027E
target P/E as we believe 2027E better reflects its earnings power after BBU capacity expansion. Our target P/E of 32x is based
on our forecast EPS CAGR of 66% in 2025-2027E and a PEG of 0.5x, which we benchmark against its major PSU partner, as we

expect Dynapack to grow alongside its partner.
Risks
Our quantitative model assigns a High Risk rating to Dynapack. However, we believe such a rating is not warranted for
Dynapack due to: 1) the company is generating solid revenue and earnings growth over next 3 years; the underlying industry,
BBU for AIDC, also enjoys secular growth; 2) the business model that involves with product customization and strict
safety/reliability requirements would imply relatively benign competition and therefore stable pricing and margins; and 3) the

company has a strong balance sheet and has been in net cash position since 2019.
Key downside risks to our price target/rating are: 1) Slower-than-expected BBU adoption in AIDC, 2) Delay in PSU customer
qualifications, 3) Competition from larger battery or power solution vendors, 4) Safety, certification and reliability issues.
Panasonic Holdings
(6752.T; ¥3957.0; 1; 16 Oct 26; 15:30)
Valuation
To derive our target price, we use a global sum-of-the-parts (SoTP) approach. In the case of Panasonic Holdings, we reference

52 other companies in the Citi coverage universe to set a fair value PER for each business division, which we sum to derive our
overall target price.
Panasonic fair-value PERs for individual business segments range from 8x to 31x, and have a weighted average of 26.2x
(excluding subsidies in the battery business), taking minority interest and equity in earnings into account. We estimate
business-derived equity value of ¥5,000/share. We calculate the present value of the amount we expect Panasonic to receive

in battery subsidies over five years at ¥400/share. Combining these two values, we derive a target price of ¥5,400.
Risks
Risks to our target price include 1) unsuccessful management policies or strategies resulting in weaker-than-expected
business investment returns or sales growth, or conversely producing greater-than-expected results at a relatively early stage,
2) stagnation in the global economy or an upturn in it, and rises or declines in demand in the electrical industry, and 3) forex

trends. If these factors manifest themselves differently than we have anticipated, the share price may vary from our target
price.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

### Page 2

2

耕興為台灣電子產品測試及驗證服務龍頭廠商：耕興成立於 1986 年，以強制性檢
測服務起家 ， 目前為台灣最大民營電子產品測試及驗證服務實驗室 ， 集檢測 、 認證 、
技術服務與零件銷售為一體，提供電子產品 EMC/Safety 等專業認證及防磁零組
件銷售，為全球少數能執行 5G sub-6GHz (FR1)、5G mmWave (FR2)、Wi-Fi 6、
Wi-Fi 6E高速行動通訊 、 無線傳輸產品且第一家取得802.11ay 與 5G NR mmWave
產品檢測認證之公司，並於台灣 /大陸/美國/德國設立實驗室。以營收結構區分，
檢測與認證收入佔營收約 90%、被動元件銷售佔 10%。國際競爭對手包含
UL/BV/PCTEST/SGS/Intertek 等、地區型競爭對手為耀登/東研信超/華測檢測/譜
尼測試等。 
2Q26 財報優於預期：耕興 2Q26 EPS 3.07 元，優於預期的 2.87 元，5G 手機、
AI NB、 企業AP 等產品檢測訂單狀況維持穩定 ， 公司1H26 5G NR市佔率 23.97%、
FCC 高階網通市占率 47.3%，維持領導地位。 
3Q26 FCC 轉單效益開始小幅貢獻：由於 FCC 擬禁止中國實驗室驗證賣往美國的
產品，公司開始收到一些高階測試的轉單，但 FCC 尚未正式全面禁止，故現在貢
獻不多，仍在等FCC 正式決策，預計11 月會有結果，目前粗估若轉單順利會有共
2~6 億元的營收貢獻，整體 3Q26 營運平穩， 預估 3Q26 營收為 12.47 億元
(+0.4%QoQ，+10.7%YoY)，毛利率 46.8%，營業利益率 30.5%，稅後淨利 2.93
億元(-6.3%QoQ，+13.9%YoY)，稅後 EPS 2.88 元。

2027 年公司若受益轉單，營運有望小幅創高：FCC 未來擬禁止所有中國實驗室為
在美國使用的電子設備 (手機、PC 等) 進行測試，研究處認為2027 年開始公司有
望受益中國實驗室受限產生的轉單效應，公司在 2027 年已有擴產計畫，地點仍待
公告，若 NTN (非地面網路)需求如預期增加，有相關設備購買需求，明年 CAPEX
有望上看 4 億台幣，整體預期營運可小幅創高，預估 2026 年營收為 49.04 億元
(+9.1%YoY)， 毛利率46.7%， 營業利益率29.3%， 稅後淨利11.39 億元(+3.9%YoY)，
稅後 EPS 11.18 元，2027 年營收為 52.04 億元(+6.1%YoY)，毛利率 47.4%，營
業利益率 31.2%，稅後淨利 12.54 億元(+10.1%YoY)，稅後 EPS 12.31 元，以過
往現金股利配發率 80%來算，預計配發 9.0 元現金股利，換算目前殖利率 4.2%，
維持買進建議，目標價 246 元(20X 2027 EPS)。

## Document 202: [000270] 기아(000270) 물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결기아(000270) 물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000270] 기아(000270) 물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결기아(000270) 물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결 - 기업.pdf

기아 (000270) 
물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결

▶Analyst 김성래 sr.kim@hanwha.com 3772-7751

Buy (유지) 
목표주가(하향): 260,000원

현재 주가(10/6) 112,600원 
상승여력 ▲130.9% 
시가총액 439,605억원 
발행주식수 390,413천주 
52주 최고가 / 최저가 206,000 / 100,600원 
90일 일평균 거래대금 1,184.39억원 
외국인 지분율 39.1%

주주 구성  
현대자동차 (외 6 인) 37.0% 
국민연금공단 (외 1 인) 6.8% 
기아차우리사주 (외 1 인) 2.3%

주가수익률(%) 1개월 3개월 6개월 12개월

상대수익률(KOSPI) -14.8  -16.1  -53.1  -87.5  
(단위: 십억 원, 원, %, 배) 
재무정보 2024 2025 2026E 2027E

영업이익 12,667  9,078  9,832  11,157  
EBITDA 15,216  11,792  13,055  14,580  
지배주주순이익 9,773  7,561  8,043  8,891  
EPS 24,575  19,457  20,765  22,942  
순차입금 -15,303  -19,162  -21,973  -24,762

배당수익률 5.8  6.0  6.2  6.7  
ROE 19.1  12.9  12.7  12.9

주가 추이

견조한 판매 확대로 매출 견조, 이익은 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 33.6조원(+17.3% 이하 Y oY)과 2.39조
원(+63.6%) 기록 전망. 3Q 매출액은 친환경차 중심 신차 확대 통해 두 자릿수 
성장세를 기록. 3Q 판매량은 도매 기준 84.3만대(+7.6% 이하 Y oY)로 전년 동
기 대비 성장 폭을 확대했고, 특히 친환경차 판매량 29.8만대(+45.9%)를 기록
하며 이번 분기 매출 성장에 절대적으로 기여.  
다만, 3Q 수익성은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입 영향과 ‘경제형EV 믹스’가 더
욱 확대(13.2% → 14.5%)되면서 단기 수익성에 부정적 작용하며 시장 기대치 
2.5조원을 다소 하회할 전망. 그러나, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮 아 지
면서 전년 동기 수 익 성 악화에 크게 영 향 을 미쳤던 판매보증비 외화환산손익 
영향이 개선(1.6%p 수준) 기대되며, 관세 부담 또한 북미 HEV 확대로 전년 동
기 대비 약 4,500억원(2.0%p) 감소하면서 부정적 수익성 영향 만회 전망.  
유럽은 점유율 확대, 북미는 수익성 개선으로 
올해 북미 HEV와 유럽 BEV 중심 신차 출시 및 물량 확대가 본격화되면
서, ’27년에는 이익 성장 기여도가 확대될 것으로 전망. 미국 조 지 아 텔 루 라 이
드 HEV 신규 생산(2.8만대) 및 HMGMA 스 포 티 지HEV신규 생산(1.0만
대) 등 수 요 가 높은 북미 현 지 에 서 HEV 물량 확대 중으로 4Q에도 텔루라이
드 HEV와 스포티지 HEV의 현지 생산/판매 확대를 비롯하여 셀토스 HEV 
출시로 ’27년 북미 중심 HEV 신차 물량 증가 및 믹스 개선 기대. 유럽은 강한 
BEV 수요가 지속되면서 중국 업체들의 공세 강화 중이나 동사의 물량 확대 또
한 견조. 3Q EV3 2.9만대, EV5 2.2만대 등 유럽 BEV 수요 대응 전략 주효. ’27
년에는 K4/셀토스 및 쏘렌토 등 HEV 신차 중 심 의 친환경차 PT 믹스 다양화
로 유럽 내 물량 확대 흐름 지속 전망 
물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결, 기말 배당 매력도 부각 
’27년 동사 영업이익 추 정 치 는 11.1조원(+13.5%)으로 증익 지속 전망. 최근 
환율 추세 고려 시 내년 수익성에 부 정 적 영향 확대 가능성 존재하나, 올해 확
인된 신차 중심 물량 확대를 통해 부정적 환율 영향을 상쇄할 것 으 로 기대. 
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-2.9%) 및 상 반 기 적 용 한 코스피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)로 목 표 주 가 를 260,000원으로 하향. 그러나, RA 설립에서의 동사 지분 
참여 및 기말 배당(’26년 DPS 7,000원 추정)에 따른 상 대 적 배당 수익 매력
도 부각 등은 주가 모 멘 텀 에 있어 기대 가능한 요소.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10월 8 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 8

기아 (000270) [한화리서치]

8 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일) 
투자등급 매수 중립 매도 합계 
금융투자상품의 비중 96.4% 3.6% 0.0% 100.0%

## Document 203: [000270] 기아(000270) 잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기기아(000270) 잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000270] 기아(000270) 잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기기아(000270) 잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

기아 (000270)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.15  
잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기

3Q26 Preview 
기아는 3Q26E 매출액 31.1조원(+8.2% YoY, -6.0% QoQ), 영업이익 2.2조원
(+53.1% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 7.2%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 
물량은 좋았으나, 아쉬운 믹스와 환율 
글로벌 도매 판매 84.7만대(+7.8% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 83.0만대
(+8.6%)를 기록했다. EV·HEV 확대에 따른 ASP 상승은 지속되고 있다. 전쟁으로 인
한 원자재 상승으로 3분기 재료비 부담은 전분기 대비 3,000억~4,000억원 증가할 
것으로 예상된다. EV 판매 비중은 2분기 13%에서 3분기 15%, 유럽은 35%에서 
43%로 확대되며 유럽 내 마진 희석은 불가피하다. 원화 강세에 따른 영업상 부담은 
판매보증충당부채 환입 등으로 상쇄돼 3분기 환율 영향은 제한적일 전망이다. 판매보
증충당부채 환입은 QoQ +5,000억원 수준으로 추정된다. 4분기부터 환율 효과는 부정
적일 것이며, 환율 10원 변동 시 연간 손익 민감도는 약 1,500억원 수준이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 165,000원으로 하향 
미국 조지아 공장의 텔루라이드 HEV(2.8만대)와 HMGMA의 스포티지 HEV(1.0만대) 
신규 생산으로 북미의 견조한 HEV 수요에 대응하며, 2027년 믹스 개선이 기대된다. 
유럽은 EV3·EV5로 점유율을 확대하는 가운데 유럽 내 할인율 하락과 EV 잔존가치 
회복으로 판매 여건이 개선되고 있다. 향후 IAA 규제에 따른 법인·플릿 혜택 제한은 
중국 업체의 원가 우위를 줄일 수 있을 전망이다. 
원화 약세 지속을 반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다.  
다만, 현재 주가는 12M Fwd P/E 기준 4.8배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 
수준, 저가 매수 기회라 판단된다. 현재 배당 수익률은 6% 수준으로, 상대적으로 높은 
배당 매력은 주가 하방을 지지할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr 
Buy (유지)

## Document 231: Advanced economies _ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Advanced economies  _ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
History tends to repeat itself in advanced economies. Once again, growth ultimately fell short of expectations by only a small
margin in the first half of 2026, despite the conflict in Iran. As early as 2025, the impact of tariffs was less severe than feared.
This is a sign that structural factors (notably AI, defence and electrification) are underpinning growth in these countries

and are expected to continue to do so in 2027. However, there are significant risks to the economic outlook, primarily of a
geopolitical nature, with a significant upside risk to inflation and interest rates. The other instance of history repeating itself
concerns a period that those under 20 cannot have experienced: the period leading up to the 2008 crisis, echoes of which can
be seen in terms of growth, inflation, the direction of monetary policy, and long-term interest rates.

Advanced economies are experiencing trends similar to those they
faced nearly 20 years ago:
- Growth remains resilient in 2025-26 despite shocks, as it did in 2006-
07, in the major advanced economies.
- Inflation is above central banks’ targets: as in 2006-07, this calls for a
tightening of monetary policy.
- In Europe and the United States, long-term interest rates have re -
turned to their 2006-07 levels (1996 in Japan): a challenging environ-

ment for public finances in a context where public debt ratios are signi-
ficantly higher than those seen 20 years ago.
THE GLOBAL ECONOMY IN THE MIDST OF A GROWTH CYCLE
We are already well into 2026, and the picture so far is clear: glo -
bal growth is proving resilient. Following a US tariff shock last year,
growth is once again proving, for the time being, to be significantly less
affected by the conflict in Iran than had been anticipated when it first

broke out. Initially, however, there were fears that this new shock would
hit growth even harder. As with the tariff hike in 2025, the impact was
measurable, particularly in terms of consumer confidence. But in both
2025 and 2026, growth has also shown a degree of resilience.
The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise
in oil prices, the recessionary impact of which is not yet apparent.
According to our forecasts, growth was expected to reach or exceed 1%

in 2026 in the four major advanced economies (the Eurozone, Japan,
the United Kingdom and the United States, where it exceeds 2%) for
the second consecutive year. This had not happened (excluding the
post-COVID catch-up period) since 2006-07. Growth figures are more
moderate (particularly for demographic reasons), but the private sec -
tor is not (at present) showing the same excesses (at that time, the
credit boom had preceded the worst crisis since the 1930s); although

debt levels have recently risen in the US tech sector.
The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial
intelligence (AI). It is fuelling global trade and business investment
in most countries. This is clearly the case in the United States, where
business investment is growing strongly as investment plans in the
US tech sector are implemented.
However, the world’s major economies are currently engaged in a
broader push to expand their capital goods investment. Alongside the

ongoing development of services—particularly digital services—we
have seen a rebound in industrial production in most countries since
early 2025. The leading sectors are primarily technology equipment,
machinery, aeronautics, defence and their essential inputs (energy and
metals). This trend is evident everywhere, including in Europe, and
is becoming entrenched over the long term, with an improvement in
business climate indicators, particularly new export orders. Growth is

therefore expected to strengthen further in 2027, particularly in the
Eurozone, where German investment plans are set to gather momen -
tum and whose spillover effects will have a greater impact on the eco-
nomy.
However, not all sectors are experiencing the same momentum. This
is particularly true for the sectors that depend on household consump-
tion, which has shown mixed trends. Our observation that consumer
spending is more buoyant in the United States than in Europe was

confirmed in 2026, and the outlook does not point to a change in this
ranking.
EDITORIAL
RESILIENCE TO SHOCK AFTER SHOCK

### Page 2

4
EcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
While European and US households are experiencing more or less the
same energy price shock, inflation remains higher in the United States.
But this has not prevented consumer spending from keeping pace, pri-
marily because it is driven by the wealthiest households, who are less
sensitive to inflation than to the wealth effect. Furthermore, while the

savings rate remains high in the Eurozone, US households have been
drawing down their savings. This is also true of British households,
which explains why the UK has seen slightly stronger growth since
the beginning of the year. In Japan, government spending has partially
offset energy inflation (while other countries are doing so to a much
lesser extent than in 2022), which has supported consumption growth.
However, these buffers—reduced savings or fiscal support—have their

limits when shocks persist. The two major current conflicts (Iran and
Ukraine) are far from being resolved, and their impact is continuing.
This persistent geopolitical risk and its implications for inflation repre-
sent a significant headwind to the growth outlook for 2027.
HIGHER INFLATION CALLS FOR MONETARY TIGHTENING
Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks
examined in this publication, which is another similarity (albeit with

different underlying causes) with the 2006-07 period. Japan is the ex -
ception, as government support measures for households are tempo -
rarily holding back inflation.
Apart from the Bank of Japan, which is engaged in a long-term norma-
lisation process, no central bank was expected to raise its key rates
in 2026. But they are now being prompted to do so for reasons that
vary from one region to another. The first is the conflict in Iran and
the resulting rise in oil prices, which triggered a rebound in energy

inflation from the second quarter onwards. However, since the conflict
was not resolved by the memorandum of understanding signed in June,
and given that the subsequent drop in oil prices proved short-lived,
inflation is expected to rise again. The rise in gas prices is expected to
exacerbate the impact, particularly in Europe, where inventories are
being replenished ahead of winter. Electricity prices are expected to
follow suit in most countries, but at a more moderate pace than in

2022. Indeed, gas is now used less to generate electricity than it was
at that time (thanks to the expansion of renewable energy), and to an
even lesser extent in France.
There is a significant risk that energy inflation will spread further to
other components of inflation in the future, and inflation expectations
could eventually reflect this. This is why the European Central Bank
has already raised its key interest rate twice (in June and September)

and, according to our scenario, will likely do so again in December. For
the same reason, we expect the Bank of England to raise its policy rate
in November. Although the cap on energy prices was maintained for
a long time, it has now been raised. As a result, inflation is expected
to rise between now and the end of the year. The aim will then be to
prevent second-round effects.
Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength. In the

United States, it has exceeded the 2% target since 2021. The main
argument in favour of a new cycle of rate hikes (following the one in
2022-2023) lies in the current resilience of growth and the improving
labour market. The Federal Reserve (Fed) must balance two equally
important objectives: maximum employment and price stability. Howe-
ver, unlike in the 4 th quarter of 2025, when the decline in job crea -
tion justified monetary easing (by 75 basis points), the current sharp

rebound in job creation completely reverses that analysis. According
to our forecasts, the unemployment rate is expected to decrease in
the coming months, which pushes back the prospect of a decline in
core inflation. This situation persists due to both supply constraints
(over which a central bank has no control) and excess demand (which
the Fed can, however, mitigate). Consequently, the Fed raised its key
rate in September and is expected to implement two further rate hikes

between now and January 2027.
In Japan, finally, while headline inflation has been brought down by
government support measures, core inflation remains significant.
Wage pressures are mounting against a backdrop of labour shortages.
The Bank of Japan is therefore being forced to accelerate the pace of its
monetary normalisation.
In all these regions, inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026
or Q1 2027. It is against this backdrop of temporary inflation that rate

hikes could also come to an end by that time (excluding Japan). Howe-
ver, this assumes that once the energy shock—the course of which
remains uncertain—subsides, inflation will fall. The effects of El Niño,
which are still difficult to gauge at present, could take over. Inflation
would then remain above the central banks’ target for a longer period.
Under this scenario, rate hikes would go further than is currently en -
visaged.
THE RISE IN LONG- TERM INTEREST RATES: A FACTOR THAT WILL WEIGH

ON PUBLIC FINANCES
Monetary policies have also to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal
policy, particularly in the United States (where the budget deficit is
high and shows no signs of shrinking) and in Japan (where the new
government is pursuing pro-growth policies). In Europe, fiscal policy
is playing a much smaller role than it did in 2022. While the stimulus
measures implemented at that time did help reduce the inflation rate
in the short term (by nearly 2.5 pp in France, for example), by keeping

demand at a high level, they delayed disinflation and increased the
scale of the monetary tightening required.
The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: it triggered a
rise in long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the
start of the year) to levels close to those of 2006-07. This rise is wides-
pread, but its causes vary. Inflation expectations appear to play a more
significant role in the Eurozone, whereas the rise in real interest rates

seems to be the main factor in the United States and Japan.

The bank
for a changing
world
Higher inflation and more resilient real growth than feared point in
the same direction: stronger than expected nominal growth that is li -
kely to be sustained. However, in the long term, high nominal growth
tends to drive up nominal interest rates (as it is a positive sign of more
profitable investment projects), in contrast to the problem of secular

stagnation raised in the 2010s (which combined low nominal growth
with low nominal interest rates).
The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period
of growth), with various situations. In several countries, the public
debt-to-GDP ratio continues to rise. In the United States, deficit reduc-
tion does not appear to be a priority, to the extent that it is expected
to remain above 6% of GDP at the very least until the end of Donald

Trump’s term of office (with little hope that the mid-term elections will
significantly alter this trend). In the United Kingdom and France, fiscal
consolidation has begun, but it is being hampered by the simultaneous
increase in certain areas of expenditure (debt servicing, defence and
healthcare, due in particular to an ageing population), which is slowing
the pace of consolidation. In Germany, the implementation of invest -
ment plans is leading to a widening of the deficit. While this does not

pose a sustainability problem, it does result in an increase in annual
debt issuance. In Japan, the budget deficit is kept in check by interest
payments that remain low, as the rise in interest rates is recent and
the average maturity of the debt is high (10 years). However, expansio-
nary fiscal policy could pose further problems in terms of debt dyna -
mics if it were to continue through the end of the decade. In Italy and
Spain, fiscal consolidation has been sufficient to stabilise the public

debt-to-GDP ratio (and even to reduce it in Spain), but the high level of
Italy’s debt service it vulnerable to a rise in interest rates.
In all these countries, the fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it
will be in 2030. Indeed, in most cases, only half of the debt was is -
sued after 2022 (that is, after the end of the zero-interest-rate period).
The debt service has so far only partially risen, except in the United
States, where most of the increase has already occurred due to a shor-

ter average debt maturity (6 years). This means that, for all countries
combined, the rate of GDP growth remains higher than the average
interest rate paid on public debt (or the effective interest rate).
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with
GDP growth. As a result, in most cases, the debt service will have re -
turned to the level as a share of GDP seen in the early 2000s. In order to
stabilise public debt-to-GDP ratios, primary balances will need to have

improved. This will even require the generation of primary surpluses,
particularly in Italy (which is already achieving this), France and the
United Kingdom. This is a crucial shift that governments will need to
introduce as early as the 2027 budget; otherwise, they risk postponing
the stabilisation of their public debt-to-GDP ratio once again.
Article completed on 17 September 2026
Stéphane Colliac
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

## Document 242: Anthropic IPO招股书八大看点：增速逆天，但风险也是明摆着的

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic IPO招股书八大看点：增速逆天，但风险也是明摆着的.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic 招股书披露多项亮点：企业客⼾规模庞⼤，收⼊超百万美元客⼾逾 1900 家；截⾄
2026 年 6 ⽉，收⼊同⽐增⻓ 1368% ，远超计算成本增速；经营现⾦流转正；全部 7 位创始⼈
仍在职。公司同时坦承⻛险，台积电供应集中及内存芯⽚短缺构成重⼤隐患。 10 ⽉ ARR 数
据将在修订版⽂件中补充披露。
摘要：
前沿 AI 模型公司 Anthropic 正式迈出上市关键⼀步，这将是 AI 领域迄今规模最⼤的 IPO 。
华尔街⻅闻提及， Anthropic 周⼀向美国证券交易委员会递交 S-1 招股说明书， 318 ⻚的⽂件披露
了其商业模式、⻛险因素及财务状况，消息提振周⼀ AI 相关芯⽚股⾛势。
招股书在第 194 ⻚预留了 10 ⽉年度经常性收⼊（ ARR ）的空⽩占位符，待修订版 S-1 中补充披
露。
资深科技财经作者 Tae Kim 发表专栏指出，这⼀安排本⾝即释放积极信号 ——若 10 ⽉ ARR 数据不
佳， Anthropic 完全没有义务将其纳⼊修订⽂件，主动预留意味着公司对该数据有信⼼。

9 ⽉ 22 ⽇， Anthropic 已发布性能⼤幅提升的 Opus 5.5 模型，另⼀款新模型 Fable 5.5 亦即将推
出。分析认为，新模型上线有望带动公司业务加速，并在 IPO 前进⼀步推升 ARR 表现。
Tae Kim 援引招股说明书，总结了⽂件中最引⼈注⽬的 8 个看点：
看点 1 ：股票代码是 ANT
我们已申请将我们的  A 类普通股在纳斯达克全球精选市场上市，股票代码为 “ANT” 。
看点 2 ： Anthropic ⼤⼒宣传其企业⽂化和员⼯留存率
作为联合创始⼈及领导者，我们⼒求实现内部彻底的坦诚，并营造了⼀种⿎励各层级员⼯之间进
⾏活跃、开放辩论的企业⽂化。
我们的⾸席执⾏官达⾥奥通常每两周都会在公司全体员⼯⾯前发表讲话，坦诚地阐述他正在考虑
的决策以及⾏业现状；其他联合创始⼈及领导者也经常在各⾃领域内进⾏类似的分享。
员⼯们会在⾯向全公司的开放论坛上，就我们复杂使命所带来的各种权衡取舍展开讨论。这种开
放的⽂化是我们⼈才密度⾼、⼈才留存率始终位居⾏业前列的原因之⼀。

我们七位创始⼈⾄今仍在公司⼯作，这使得 Anthropic 成为唯⼀⼀家保留了全部创始团队的⼤型
⼈⼯智能公司。
看点 3 ：企业级应⽤正经历着惊⼈的指数级收⼊增⻓。

### Page 2

企业客⼾采⽤我们产品的速度和深度有⼒地证明了  Claude 在⼈⼯智能领域的前沿地位。截⾄
2026 年  9 ⽉  30 ⽇，我们的客⼾群包括：
•过去 12 个⽉收⼊超过 10 万美元的客⼾超过 11900 家； • 过去 12 个⽉收⼊超过 100 万美元的客⼾超
过 1900 家；以及
•过去 12 个⽉收⼊超过 100 万美元的客⼾约 250 家。

### Page 3

看点 4 ：这些⾃我强化的改进循环是他们的竞争优势
改进循环。 Claude 在企业级部署中的应⽤，加深了我们对客⼾最为关注的难题、边缘案例和特
定领域挑战的理解。我们根据客⼾在⽣产环境中使⽤我们产品的反馈，改进我们的模型、平台和
应⽤程序。这些改进促成了更⼴泛的部署和应⽤，从⽽推动下⼀轮改进。
投资循环。企业级应⽤拓展带来的收⼊，有助于我们为  Claude 的改进提供所需的计算、研究和
⼈才⽀持。
看点 5 ：存储芯⽚供应和台积电晶圆分配都是很⼤的⻛险
⼈⼯智能⼯作负载所需的专⽤芯⽚，特别是⾼性能⼈⼯智能加速器和内存芯⽚，由少数⼏家制造
商⽣产。先进芯⽚的⽣产极其复杂，需要尖端的技术⼯艺。全球⼤部分最先进的芯⽚制造能⼒集
中在中国台湾，主要集中在台积电（ TSMC ）。这种地域集中带来了重⼤⻛险，包括易受地震、
台⻛等⾃然灾害的影响，以及亚洲地缘政治紧张局势。
此外，由于对先进芯⽚制造的需求，台积电有时⽆法完全满⾜市场对芯⽚制造能⼒的需求，因
此，我们并⾮总能及时获得⾜够的芯⽚。
因此，任何对中国台湾或台积电先进芯⽚供应的限制或延误都将对我们的业务、财务状况和经营

业绩产⽣不利影响。此外，⾼性能⼈⼯智能加速器的关键组件 —— ⾼带宽内存，以及计算基础设
施中使⽤的其他⾼性能内存产品，主要由 SK 海⼒⼠、三星电⼦和美光科技⽣产。这些制造商都
在努⼒扩⼤产能以满⾜需求。然⽽，这些努⼒能否迅速实现充⾜的供应以满⾜需求，尤其是在⼤
型客⼾或竞争对⼿获得独家供应承诺或以其他⽅式预订了制造商的⼏乎所有可⽤产能的情况下，
这⼀点⽆法保证。
看点 6 ：推理成本每年都⼤幅下降
即便我们加快了研发和产品迭代速度，并扩⼤了内部  Claude 的使⽤范围，每美元计算成本带来
的收 ⼊ 也 显 著 增 ⻓ 。 例 如 ， 截 ⾄  2026 年  6 ⽉  30 ⽇ 的 三 个 ⽉ 内 ， 我 们 的收 ⼊ 同 ⽐ 增 ⻓
1368% ，⽽同期计算和基础设施⽀出仅增⻓了  300% 。
在满⾜不断增⻓的客⼾需求的同时，我们还凭借  Mythos 5 、 Fable 5 和  Opus 5 等领先的新模
型，在前沿研究领域取得了重⼤进展，并推出了  Claude Cowork 和  Claude Design 等新产品。

看点 8 ：经营现⾦流为正

## Document 246: Anthropic七位创始人近乎平分股权，IPO后持股价值或高达2000亿美元

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic七位创始人近乎平分股权，IPO后持股价值或高达2000亿美元.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic 即将 IPO ，七位联合创始⼈⼏乎平分股权，各⾃持股价值最⾼达 300 亿美元，合
计约 2000 亿美元。这⼀罕⻅平等架构打破硅⾕⾦字塔式薪酬惯例，七⼈还承诺捐出 80% 股
票，但受锁定期约束，最早上市六个⽉后⽅可部分变现。
摘要：
七位创始⼈平分巨额股权，打破硅⾕惯例的薪酬架构，令这场 AI 独⻆兽上市备受瞩⽬。
据 Fortune 分析，Anthropic 即将启动 IPO ，七位联合创始⼈各⾃持有的股权在公司上市后合计
价值最⾼可达 2000 亿美元。若按部分媒体报道所述的 1.5 万亿⾄ 2 万亿美元估值区间计算，每位
创始⼈的持股价值约在 210 亿⾄ 300 亿美元之间。值得关注的是，包括 CEO Dario Amodei 和总裁
兼董事会主席  Daniela Amodei 在内的七⼈，持股数量⼏乎完全相同，彻底打破了⼤型科技公司
普遍采⽤的 " ⾦字塔式 " 薪酬结构。
这⼀架构对投资者⽽⾔意义重⼤。七位创始⼈均被列为 IPO 的售股⽅，但受锁定期约束，最早须

在上市约六个⽉后⽅可出售部分股份，余下股份需待约⼀年后才可变现。此外，七⼈已承诺将
各⾃ 80% 的股票捐作慈善，若兑现，捐赠总额可能⾼达 800 亿⾄ 1650 亿美元，尽管该承诺⽬前不
具法律约束⼒。
近乎平等的股权分配，硅⾕罕⻅先例
据 Fortune 分析，每位联合创始⼈持有的股份数量介于 2.31 亿⾄ 2.44 亿股之间，并且每⼈持有完
全相同的 2,185,400 份可⽴即⾏权的股票期权，以及将在 IPO 后归属的 639,500 份限制性股票单位
（ RSU ）。按此计算，七⼈各⾃约持有公司 1.4% ⾄ 1.5% 的股权，合计持股价值约 2000 亿美元。
薪酬咨询公司 Farient Advisors 创始⼈ Robin Ferracone 表⽰， Anthropic 对创始团队采取的 " 平等
主义⽅式 " ⼗分罕⻅，七⼈的创始团队规模本⾝也属 " 相对较⼤ " 。 " 给所有创始⼈相同的东西，这
本⾝就不寻常， " 她说， " 通常随着归属周期推进，薪酬会逐渐与职位挂钩⽽产⽣分化。 "

Anthropic 在周⼀提交的招股说明书中将这⼀创始团队描述为 " 独特具备使命管理能⼒的群体 " ，
同时特别指出 Dario 和 Daniela 对于 " 制定公司战略⽅向⾄关重要 " ，且 " 难以替代 " 。
Amodei 兄妹：同等薪酬，同步晋升
招股⽂件显⽰，由于两位 Amodei 是公司认定的仅有的执⾏官，其薪酬信息得以完整披露。去
年，兄妹⼆⼈各⾃获得 654,167 美元的年薪，加上⽂件中估值约合计 1300 万美元的股票和期权
—— 若按 2 万亿美元估值折算，则价值约 3.3 亿美元。今年 7 ⽉，两⼈均获加薪，年薪升⾄ 140 万
美元。
今年 2 ⽉， Anthropic 向两⼈各授予约 140 万份股票期权及 240 万份 RSU ， 7 ⽉再追加 53.7 万份
RSU 。上述 RSU 将在 Anthropic 上市后以季度为单位分批归属，前提是两⼈仍在职。按 2 万亿美
元估值计算，上述授予对两⼈⽽⾔各价值约 5.05 亿美元。
公司在注册声明中解释道： " 我们在制定⾼管薪酬时会将内部薪酬平等纳⼊考量。每位联合创始

⼈ 获 得 相 同 的 薪 酬 。 " 其 余 五 位联 合 创 始 ⼈ — — To m  B r o w n 、 J a r e d  K a p l a n 、 S a m
McCandlish 、 Jack Clark 和 Chris Olah—— 均持有⾸席计算官、⾸席科学官等 C 级职位，但未出
现在公司薪酬表中。不过，七⼈持有完全相同数量的期权和 RSU ，归属时间均不晚于 11 ⽉底，
与已披露的两位 Amodei 的授予情况⾼度吻合，暗⽰其余五⼈获得了同等授予。

Spark Capital 的普通合伙⼈，其旗下基⾦⾃ 2023 年以来已累计向 Anthropic 投资 7.77 亿美元。
Anthropic 尚未为 IPO 定价，具体细节仍可能发⽣变化。

## Document 290: EcoNews of 26 October 2026 (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/EcoNews of 26 October 2026 (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.39 / 26 October 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
In brief. The week was dominated by a continued global selloff in tech
stocks, leading to safe haven flows into US Treasuries, Japanese Go -
vernment bonds and German bunds. As a result, their yields fell des -
pite energy prices surging to or past their Spring highs. Economic data
generally showed continued resilience. French bonds were under rising

pressure, but Moody's unexpected decision to maintain its France ra -
ting unchanged could, at the margin, help stabilise the spread. The Fed,
ECB and BOJ should be on hold next week while signaling further rate
action is likely.
EUROPEAN UNION
A win for securitization. The Council and the European Parliament have
reached a provisional political agreement on measures to revive and
simplify the EU’s securitization market. The updated framework will

reduce banks’ capital requirements for the safest and most well-struc-
tured transactions, notably for a newly introduced “resilient” category
of securitization. As a key component of the “One Europe, One Market”
initiative and the EU’s Savings and Investment Union agenda, this aims
to channel increased lending to households and businesses. The rele -
vant legal texts must now be finalised.
EUROZONE
Expansion continues. The flash PMIs weakened in October, mainly on

the back of a decline in France. The EZ composite index dipped to 51.9
(-1.2 m/m), dragged down by services (51.7, -1.3 m/m), although ma -
nufacturing also dipped (52.4, -0.5 m/m). Both remain in expansion
territory, and manufacturing new export orders rose to their highest
level since early 2022. Price signals were also rather reassuring, with
the services output price index easing to 54.9 (-1.7 m/m). Households
are more cautious: consumer confidence fell for a second consecutive

month to a five-month low (-17.9). Households’ inflation expectations
edged up at short horizons (+0.1 pp to 3.6% at one year and 3.1% at
three years), but long-term expectations remained anchored, with the
five-year median stable at 2.5%.
France
Rising market stress despite decent economic news; a reprieve from
Moody’s. The spread on French 10-year bond relative to Germany’s
peaked to 174 bps on Friday, the highest since 2011 and only 16 bps
short of its all-time high, as markets price in anticipation of worst-case

scenarios regarding the country’s ability to repair its public finances.
The 10Y yield briefly exceeded 5.1% before falling back to 5% (above
the US equivalent). Nonetheless, services output was stable in August,
following a 0.6% m/m increase in July, a much higher pace than in Q2.
The rebound is driven by the catering sector, the digital sector, business
services, and real estate. The INSEE business climate improved from
96 to 97 (long-term average = 100) in October, but the survey was

conducted before the riots. The main improvement was in manufactu-
ring, which rose from 101 to 105, driven by increased new orders in the
aeronautics sector, and to a lesser extent by the services sector (from
98 to 99). The PMI survey (conducted partially after the riots) was
less positive: the composite index fell to 48.4 (-2.7 m/m), with a sharp
decline in services (48.4; -2.8 m/m) and a slightly smaller decline in
manufacturing (49.3; -1.3 m/m). Moody's unexpectedly announced af -

ter the market close on Friday that it was maintaining France’s ra -
ting at Aa3 on account of France’s diversified economic structure and
sound public institutions, but maintained its negative outlook. This is
on par with the UK and one notch above S&P and Fitch. Moody’s made
clear keeping this rating is conditional on a budget being adopted that
reduces the fiscal deficit, along with an easing of social and market
stress going forward. Saturday’s demonstrations across the country

saw strong attendance but only limited violent incidents.
Germany
Prices continue to rise, but confidence surveys remain on an upward
trend. Producer prices rose 6.7% y/y in September (+2.1pp m/m; the
highest level in 3.5 years) due to higher prices for energy and inter -
mediate goods, primarily metals. The ZEW Economic Sentiment Index
improved beyond consensus forecasts in October and returned to its
highest level since May 2023 (-35.9; +11.2 m/m), albeit the Economic

Expectations Index fell to its lowest level since July (28.1; -6.6 m/m).
The recovery in economic activity continues, although a slowdown is
evident: the flash Composite PMI slowed (52.8; -1 m/m; the lowest in
2 months) due to weaker growth in the services sector (51.7; -1.1 m/m;
the lowest in 2 months) and in manufacturing output (55.1; -0.9 m/m;
the lowest in 3 months). Nevertheless, in both sectors, hiring is rising
slightly, supported by growth in export sales and the spillover effects of

investment plans. Despite persistently high input prices, selling prices
are slowing (reaching their lowest level since February in the manu -
facturing sector).
UNITED KINGDOM
Inflation, wage growth and activity accelerate. CPI inflation overshot
consensus on energy prices, printing at 0.4% m/m and 3.5% y/y in Sep-
tember (consensus: 3.4%, prior: 3.1%), its highest in 11 months. Core
CPI came in at 2.8% y/y (up 0.2 pp), in line with consensus. Pressures

remained persistent according to the PPI at 8.3% y/y (prior: 6.7%). La -
bour market and activity data also surprised positively. Payroll growth
reached 102k in August (3m/3m), bringing the unemployment rate to
4.7% (down 0.2 pp), its lowest since July 2025. October PMI surveys
beat expectations in both manufacturing (52.8, consensus: 51.8, prior:
51.9) and services (53.1, consensus: 51.8, prior: 52.1). The only negative
news came from headline retail sales, falling 0.4% m/m in September,

with the core gauge (excluding petrol) deteriorating by -0.7% m/m.
UNITED STATES
Construction struggling, GENIUS Act implemented. Housing starts
dropped -5.2% m/m in September (-7.4% y/y). This third consecutive
monthly decline dragged the Q3 average to its lowest since Q2 2020.
Building permits also came in negative, at -0.2% m/m (-3.0% y/y), while
the NAHB homebuilder sentiment index plunged to its weakest since
2012. The average 30-year fixed mortgage rate eased to 7.32% (still

134bp above pre-war level), as the ongoing AI selloff pushed down
US treasury yields. The GENIUS Act final implementation rule from the
Office of the Comptroller of the Currency’s (OCC) leaves the door open
for stablecoin customers to receive yield or rewards. Issuers remain
prohibited from paying holders any form of interest or yield solely
in connection with the holding, use, or retention of payment stable -
coins. This represents a shift from the OCC’s February proposal and

has renewed concerns within the banking industry about deposit flight.
The rule will take effect on January 18, 2027.
JAPAN
Activity and inflation keep rising. Composite PMI was unchanged in
September at 52.3, solidly in expansion territory, as an improvement
in services activity offset slower output growth in manufacturing. The
Manufacturing PMI disappointed at 53.3 (-0.8 m/m; an 8-month low).
Underlying price pressures, meanwhile, extended. Headline CPI was

7
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.39 / 26 October 2026

flat on a monthly basis, bringing the annual rate up to 2.1% (up 0.4 pp).
Core CPI (excl. fresh food) matched it at 2.1% y/y (up 0.4 pp), in line
with consensus. More importantly, the New Core index, which strips
out fresh food and energy, rose sharply to 2.4% y/y (up 0.5 pp), its
fastest pace since January.
EMERGING ECONOMIES

ASIA
China
New monetary policy tool and export controls. The central bank’s Go-
vernor Pan Gongsheng unveiled a plan to switch (“when appropriate”)
its key policy rate from the 7-day reverse repo rate (at 1.4% since May
2025) to the overnight reverse repo rate, in order to reflect develop -
ments in the interbank market. New export controls: The government
plans to add 40 key technologies to the list of technologies prohibited
or restricted for export, including in the AI (for ex. LLMs, computing

clusters), biotechnology and photovoltaic sectors.
Indonesia
BI kept policy rate unchanged at 5.75%, raised its deposit facility rate
by 25bp to 5% and announced a phased cut in reserve requirements
from 9% to 7% by December. This combination of limited FX support
and measures to support credit growth sends mixed signals amidst
pressures on the rupiah following MSCI’s downgrade warning and un-
dermines the central bank’s credibility. The rupiah has lost 1% against

the USD since mid-October, with little contagion effect on other Asian
currencies.
South Korea
The Bank of Korea (BoK) kept its policy rate unchanged at 3.0%, but
the tightening cycle may not be over. CPI inflation was 2.9% y/y in Sep-
tember, above the central bank’s target of 2%, while high oil prices,
rapid growth in Seoul housing prices and wage pressures (though
concentrated in the electronics sector) raise the risk of lasting infla -
tion pressure. As economic activity remains robust, supported by strong

exports and recovering investment, the BoK has scope to raise rates
further.
EMEA
Hungary
Monetary status quo and 2027 budget in consolidation mode. The
Central Bank kept its policy rate unchanged at 5.5%, as expected. A cau-
tious stance will likely prevail in the short term. Inflation has been
picking up in recent months, albeit mildly, and will continue to move
upward as the government recently removed the margin cap on fuel.
Separately, the Budget for 2027 and the medium-term fiscal plan show

Hungary’s goal to join the Eurozone; the government expects to reduce
the fiscal deficit to 4.9% of GDP in 2027 (from 7% expected in 2026) and
towards 2.8% of GDP by 2030.
Türkiye
Cautious monetary loosening. The Central Bank lowered its policy rate
by 100 bp to 36% at its October Monetary Policy Committee meeting,
in line with market expectations. The overnight lending and borrowing
rates were also reduced by 100 bp, to 39% and 34.5%, respectively.

The improvement in underlying inflation has yet to translate into a
sustained period of low inflation, justifying the CBRT’s preference for a
measured pace of monetary easing.
LATIN AMERICA
Brazil
President Lula da Silva was re-elected on Sunday for a fourth term
(with 51.8% of the vote), defeating far right-wing candidate Flavio Bol-
sonaro (48.2%). Lula will enter this new term facing significant econo -
mic and political challenges. Public debt has risen to more than 82% of

GDP , while high borrowing costs and rigid mandatory spending leave
limited room to reconcile fiscal consolidation with social spending and
investment. Restoring confidence in the fiscal outlook will be a key
challenge in the short term.
COMMODITIES
Pressure rising. Amid growing rumours of imminent re-escalation of
military operations in the Gulf, energy prices rose sharply this week,
with brent touching $117/b – near its April peak, and European gas

prices (TTF) rising to 97 €/MWh on Monday, their highest level since
Dec 2022. The Asian gas benchmark (JKM) is following the same trend
(4.6% w/w to 31.4 $/mbtu, equivalent to around 96 €/MWh). Diesel
crack spreads are slightly decreasing in Europe (-2.8% w/w on Monday)
and in the US (-4% w/w on Monday). The first Russian diesel tanker
intended to the US market left Russia this Friday and should supply the
US market in 2 weeks. US diesel average retail prices remain oriented

to the upside (0.7% w/w at 6.32 $/gallon on Monday). US commercial
crude inventories fell by 3.1 mb in the week ending 16 October, signi -
ficantly exceeding market expectations for a 340,000-barrel decline.

## Document 304: Financial conditions in the Eurozone_ a tightening temporarily tempered by credit volumes (2026-10-07)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Financial conditions in the Eurozone_ a tightening temporarily tempered by credit volumes (2026-10-07).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

7 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FINANCIAL CONDITIONS IN THE EUROZONE: A TIGHTENING TEMPORARILY TEMPERED BY CREDIT VOLUMES
Thomas Humblot
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
Since June 2026, the ECB has been publishing 1 a new indicator that helps to assess the transmission of its monetary policy to the economy:
the Broad Intermediation Gauge 2 (ECB-BIG). This new tool has revealed that, thus far, strong credit growth has limited the restrictive impact

of tightening financial conditions caused by rising interest rates in the Eurozone. However, this offsetting effect is beginning to fade.
The ECB-BIG: a new indicator for a broader view of financial conditions
The ECB-BIG complements the range of indicators used by the European Central Bank (ECB) to assess the effective transmission of its monetary
policy. This new tool provides a comprehensive view of how restrictive (or expansive) financial conditions are in the Eurozone. It stands out

for its approach that focuses more specifically on intermediation conditions. Therefore, the ECB-BIG provides a more comprehensive view than
most other financial condition indicators of financial conditions. In fact, it covers variables relating to the volume of bank lending to households
and businesses (the ‘volume’ component). Traditionally, this type of indicator tends to focus more on market variables (primarily interest and
exchange rates), share prices and corporate spreads. In addition, the ‘price’ component of the ECB-BIG is extended to include the cost of bank

loans, which is another new feature.
The rise in interest rates was temporarily offset by increased lending
By aggregating the variables relating to banking and non-banking intermediation conditions, the ECB-BIG shows that rising interest rates
(negative price effect) were offset in particular by lending volumes, which have continued to rise in 2026 (positive volume effect). A more
‘traditional’ indicator of financial conditions, the Macro-Finance FCI (MF-FCI) — which is, incidentally, included in the ECB-BIG — reveals a

much more pronounced tightening over the same period (see the orange curve in the chart). However, this offsetting of the price effect by the
volume effect is now beginning to wane, a sign that monetary policy is being effectively transmitted to the economy. Financial intermediaries
are gradually passing on the rise in rates to their customers (see the dotted green lines, which represent the composite indicators of the cost
of new loans to households and businesses, combining interest rates for different maturities), while lending volumes are falling. As a result,

between July and August 2026, seasonally adjusted flows of loans to households and businesses almost halved.
1 P . Lane, 2026, The Outlook for the Euro Area Economy, Background slides for Reuters NEXT Europe, London, 16 June 2026
2 A. Allayioti et al., 2026, The ECB-BIG index: tracking credit conditions in the euro area, ECB Economic Bulletin, Issue 5/2026
SOURCE: ALLAYIOTI AND AL. (2026), ECB, BLETZINGER ET AL. (2026), BNP PARIBAS ESTIMATES

In the longer term, financial conditions are expected to tighten, including as measured by the ECB-BIG
Despite the very sharp widening of sovereign spreads between 28 September and 2 October, financial conditions as measured by the
MF-FCI did not tighten, with sovereign yields actually falling over this period (with the exception of the OAT and the BTP), for example.

Nevertheless, the high volatility of the bond market, particularly in the sovereign segment, is likely to continue to affect financial
conditions. Looking slightly further ahead, the ECB’s key interest rate rises — the one that occurred on 16 September and another that we
anticipate for December 2026 (both +25 bp) — are likely to result in a more pronounced tightening. With a time lag of a number of weeks,
financial conditions, as defined by the ECB-BIG, are also expected to tighten. The price effect is likely to lead to a negative volume effect

on this occasion.

## Document 311: France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08)

Source PDF: all report pdf/France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 8 October 2026

EcoFlash

France: double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and 
stable non-performing loans ratio

Business creations rose by 12% y/y over the first eight months of 2026 1. This is significantly higher than the 5.3% y/y 
growth recorded in 2025. This increase was driven by sectors linked to information and communication technologies.

Meanwhile, business bankruptcies rose by 5.3% y/y between January and July, according to data from the Banque de 
France (compared with the 3.6% y/y increase for the whole of 2025). The rise was more pronounced in the 1st quarter (+8% y/y) 
before slowing down. This slowdown could persist, as, according to Altares, payment delays have fallen to an average of 12.3 
days in the second quarter of 2026, compared with 14.1 days a year earlier (and 13.1 days for the European average).

Rising bankruptcies are more heavily affecting the services sector, which is also experiencing slightly stronger growth 
in business creations. This correlation is particularly noticeable in the retail sector, as business creations have risen by 13% 
since the start of 2026, while bankruptcies are up by 3%. In construction and manufacturing, business bankruptcies have been 
falling since the second quarter of 2026.

The average ratio of non -performing corporate loans has remained stable at a moderately high level for the past year  
(4.0% of total loans and advances in Q2 2026). In construction, where activity is more cyclical than in other sectors, the ra tio of 
non-performing loans has reached its highest level since 2019, standing at 9.5%.

The proportion of loans that have deteriorated but are not yet non-performing (“Stage 2” according to IFRS 9 terminology) 
is stable, standing at 15.4% q/q, and remains below its all -time high (16.1%). This indicator, which helps to anticipate trends in 
non-performing loans, is relatively encouraging.

Despite a significant increase since 2021, the economic weight of bankruptcies remains, on average, below its peak in 
Q1 2025 (0.53 compared with 0.61, respectively). This indicator generally allows bankruptcies to be weighted based on the size 
of the businesses. However, the economic weight of bankrupted small businesses is at a historically high level (2.11).

Take a look at our EcoCharts on trends in business creations and bankruptcies in France and the 
associated risks. It will be updated on a quarterly basis.

Thomas Humblot 
Economiste, team: Banking economics 
thomas.humblot@bnpparibas.com

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of Advanced economies, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

1 For more details, see Business creations and bankruptcies in France: double-digit growth in business creations, lower growth in bankruptcies, 
and stable non-performing loans, 8 October 2026.

## Document 363: Postcard from Bangkok IMF meetings_ don’t take resilience for granted (2026-10-19)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Postcard from Bangkok IMF meetings_ don’t take resilience for granted (2026-10-19).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.38 / 19 October 2026EDITORIAL
POSTCARD FROM BANGKOK IMF MEETINGS: DON’T TAKE RESILIENCE FOR GRANTED
1 See EcoNews for more details.
The global economy’s unexpected resilience might have invited the self-congratulation that is a trademark of internatio-
nal official meetings. But policymakers gathered in Bangkok for the 2026 IMF Annual Meetings were wearily aware that
“continued resilience cannot be taken for granted”, as noted in the prologue to the closing Chair’s Statement to the Inter-

national Monetary and Financial Committee meeting. Growth forecasts remain in line with those made a year ago, when
interest rates were much lower and no energy shock was in the cards, and even with those made in April, when a short
shock was the base case. But what has sustained global growth against the odds so far could falter before the headwinds
ease: oil buffers are being depleted; fiscal policy urgently needs to shift from support to deficit reduction as interest rates

remain persistently higher; and the AI boom that has been a salvatory growth engine comes with serious questions about
its sustainability and long-term economic effects. In addition to these global issues, the combination of fiscal challenges
and elections in France and the US were seen as key risks, while some topics that had until recently been in the spotlight
found themselves overshadowed.
BETTER GROWTH THAN EXPECTED FOR MOST , WORSE INFLATION FOR ALL

Despite the energy shock and surge in interest rates, the IMF's
latest global growth forecast is unchanged from a year ago and April
— at 3.1% in 2026 and 3.4% in 2027 — although the geographical
breakdown has shifted: war-exposed countries and many energy im -
porters have been downgraded, while AI-exposed countries have been
upgraded. Asia is the standout winner, expected to account for 68% of
global growth in 2026 — a feat for a region highly dependent on Gulf

energy. Compared to July, the growth forecast for advanced economies
is revised up to 2.1% in 2026 (+0.4pp, driven by Germany) and remains
at 1.8% in 2027. Among emerging markets, the IMF upgraded Asia and
downgraded other regions. Meanwhile, global headline inflation is ex-
pected to rise to 4.7% in 2026, mainly due to energy and food, before
easing to 4.1% in 2027 (revised +0.2pp)1.
AI OPTIMISM DOMINATES, BUT WITH SOME HEAVY UNANSWERED
QUESTIONS

The topic of AI dominated the Meetings. The broad consensus was that
it is transformational and offers great opportunities to all countries.
But, for now, it is producing a mixed bag of economic effects: a power-
ful boost to global demand, directly through an investment and trade
boom and indirectly through wealth effects from stock-market gains;
but also much higher interest rates (fuelled by the borrowing needed
to fund the AI build-out, and higher prices for chips, all the products

they go into, and the electricity and water data centres need).
Beyond this shared diagnosis, strong arguments could be heard on both
sides of several critical questions. The most pressing being: will AI ge-
nerate earnings commensurate with current expectations? If not — or
even if expectations are merely downgraded — expect large declines in
AI-related asset prices, a sudden stop in funding and a significant blow
to growth wherever AI has been a key engine. This is not in anyone’s

baseline for next year, but it is clearly a top risk. To what extent will AI
boost productivity over five or ten years? The IMF incorporates almost
no boost in its five-year baseline, but presents an upside scenario in
which it does. Will AI create more jobs than it destroys, raise or reduce
labour’s share of income, prove inflationary or deflationary beyond the
near term, and strengthen cyber resilience or turbocharge attacks?
And how should it be taxed to preserve the fiscal base and offset unde-

sirable labour-market effects?
A MIXED POLICY SCORECARD
Monetary policy has appropriately taken a hawkish turn nearly eve -
rywhere, and an increase in rate rises is likely in key economies, though
not enough to threaten growth. Eurozone central bankers were parti -
cularly clear in sticking to their “moderate response” playbook.
Fiscal policy, however, has yet to adjust to the end of cheap debt. With
public debt high in most countries, a growing share of national bud -

gets will be absorbed by interest payments. Yet very few countries are
consolidating. The IMF’s top call to action was to address this urgent-
ly, especially among the worst offenders — the US and France. Fiscal
restraint would also help central banks curb inflation, reducing the
need for higher interest rates.
Policies to redress global imbalances are not closer to being adop -
ted than at previous IMF Meetings, but they were not particular -
ly highlighted by the IMF this time, beyond the mantra that “the US

needs to save more, China to consume more, and Europe to invest
more”. There were reportedly good discussions behind closed doors
— at the G7 on “non-market policies and practices (NMPPs)” — and
encouraging early steps in a long journey in Europe-China talks. That
is better than nothing, but hardly enough to expect progress within a
reasonable timeframe. Meanwhile, the risks of a disorderly unwind will
keep rising.
TWO ZONES OF CONCERN IN FOCUS: FRANCE AND THE US

France was a top concern for many participants. They feared a repeat
of the 2010–2012 Eurozone debt crisis, seeing a country with public
debt approaching 120% of GDP , sub-par growth, a poor record of fiscal
consolidation, riots in the streets and presidential elections six mon -
ths away, with populist candidates leading the polls. French and Eu -
ropean policymakers, alongside private-sector participants, presented
a different picture, namely: a fiscal situation that is serious, but is

being managed (the draft budget provides for meaningful adjustment
and is very likely to be adopted without being weakened); a widening
consensus from centre-left to far-right which favours a return to fiscal
responsibility, in line with a similar shift in public opinion; and the
electoral process is only beginning, with the outcome still wide open —
not least because the candidates’ names and numbers are not known.

### Page 2

4
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.38 / 19 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
It was also pointed out that the quantum of fiscal adjustment required
is not out of line with previous consolidation episodes in France in the
2010s and the 1990s. Still, most participants left not much reassured
and expecting a bumpy ride ahead.
US participants outside government saw a growing likelihood of a De -
mocratic sweep in the midterm elections, Either way, they expected

little fiscal consolidation, despite a manifestly challenging fiscal path
that may end up causing volatility in financial markets. Non-US parti -
cipants also feared growing policy unpredictability.
OVERSHADOWED TOPICS: THE STARS OF RECENT IMF MEETINGS
Tariffs were entirely absent from the agenda and conversations. Toke-
nised finance (rather than the narrower topic of stablecoins) still fea -
tured, but far less prominently: as the field takes shape, discussion

has shifted from the conceptual what and why to the technical how.
Climate change and low-income countries remained on the agenda,
but far less in focus than at previous Meetings. The sheer number of
issues demanding attention may be overwhelming financial policyma-
kers’ bandwidth.
*****
All in all, while the global context remains extraordinarily challenging,
it’s comforting to see that global financial policy makers are broadly
alert to both risks and opportunities, and to what needs to be done for

the latter to prevail. Now they need to deliver.
Isabelle Mateos y Lago
isabelle.mateosylago@bnpparibas.com

## Document 384: Taiwan_ AI, energy and 4Q outlook (2026-10-13)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Taiwan_ AI, energy and 4Q outlook (2026-10-13).pdf
Report source: DBS
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

• The global landscape remains complex, shaped by AI, 
energy prices and tariffs. 
• Taiwan’s AI cycle remains strong, with robust AI 
exports and investment and an intact semiconductor 
competitive moat. 
• AI is generating broader domestic spillovers, with 
wealth effects and wage growth supporting 
consumption. 
• Taiwan is cushioning the energy shock by diversifying 
energy imports and absorbing much of the increase in 
upstream costs. 
• AI-driven growth is likely to moderate from 4Q26 into 
2027, as hyperscaler  cash-flow pressures rise and 
higher global rates lift investment costs. 
• Energy remains a n inflation risk, with domestic 
electricity prices facing upward pressure after the 
November local elections. 
• GDP growth is expected to moderate to high -single 
digits from 4Q26, while we maintain our 11.6%/5.6% 
GDP forecasts for 2026/27. 
• Inflation is likely to remain above 2% in 4Q26, with our 
2.0%/2.1% CPI forecasts for 2026/27 unchanged. 
• We maintain our call for CBC to hike 12.5bp to 2.125% 
in December, amid tighter liquidity conditions. 
• Housing should recover gradually despite credit 
easing, as excess new -home inventory remains a n 
overhang.

Taiwan: AI, energy and 4Q outlook

13 October 2026

Ma Tieying, CFA                        
Senior Economist 
matieying@dbs.com

### Page 2

Taiwan: AI, energy and 4Q outlook October 13, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

Global backdrop

The global landscape remains complex, 
shaped by the interplay of AI, energy 
prices and tariffs, with significant 
implications for growth, inflation and 
financial conditions.

1) AI

The debate over the durability of the AI 
investment boom continues.  Tailwinds 
include technological advances, broader 
adoption, and s trategic and geopolitical 
considerations. Headwinds range from 
uncertainty over investment returns  and 
hyperscaler financing pressure to 
physical bottlenecks , safety concerns, 
and regulatory uncertainty.

Hyperscaler financing pressure has 
attracted increasing attention . In the 
early stages of the AI investment boom, 
hyperscaler capex was largely financed 
by internal cash flow. This is now 
changing, as free cash flow is expected 
to turn negative from 4Q, requiring major 
players to rely on debt issuance and 
other external financing increasingly.

At the same time, higher global interest 
rates are raising the cost of AI 
investment. The Fed resumed rate hikes 
at its September meeting, while global 
bond yields remain under upward 
pressure amid persistent inflation and 
elevated public and private borro wing 
needs. Our baseline assumes US rates 
will rise by 50bps this year and a further 
25bps in 2027 to 4.50%, with global 
long-term rates also remaining under 
pressure. This could increasingly weigh 
on the economics and pace of AI 
investment.

US-China AI competition is also drawing 
increasing attention.  Across the five 
layers of the AI ecosystem, China is 
making rapid progress in energy, 
infrastructure, AI models and 
applications, supported by its large scale, 
cost-effective domestic manufacturing 
supply chains and huge consumer 
market, which provides a wide range of 
real-world scenarios for AI adoption. The 
emergence of a more integrated and 
cost-competitive Chinese AI ecosystem 
could increasingly challe nge the US 
ecosystem, which currently operates at a 
relatively higher cost.

China, however, remains relatively weak 
in semiconductors.  Around half of 
China's domestic AI chip market still 
relies on foreign suppliers, including 
lower-end NVIDIA chips. This 
semiconductor bottleneck remains a key 
constraint on China's AI development. In 
our view, Taiwanese semiconductor 
companies' competiti ve moat therefore 
remains largely intact in the near term.

200

### Page 3

Taiwan: AI, energy and 4Q outlook October 13, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

So far, there is limited evidence of a 
significant slowdown in AI investment 
or semiconductor demand.  Hyperscaler 
capex maintained robust yoy growth 
through 2Q, while Bloomberg forecasts 
growth to moderate from 95% this year 
to 39% in 2027. Similarly, global 
semiconductor sales remained strong 
through 3Q, while WSTS expects growth 
to moderate from 90% this year to 27 % 
in 2027.

2) Energy

The US -Iran conflict remains at a 
standoff, keeping the energy -price 
outlook highly uncertain.  New 
developments are likely after the US 
midterm elections in November, with 
both further escalation and de -
escalation possible. Crude oil prices have 
remained elevated at around 
USD100/bbl amid a persistent 
geopolitical risk premium . In contrast, 
refined product prices have risen more 
sharply as refining disruptions and 
tighter product logistics have amplified 
the supply shock. Natural gas prices 
have also increased sharply, reflecting 
disruptions to LNG supply and limited 
alternative shipping routes.

Taiwan has been actively diversifying 
its energy import sources to cushion the 
impact. LNG imports from Qatar have 
fallen to zero since April, while imports 
from the US and other suppliers have 
surged to offset the shortfall, helping 
maintain stable domestic electricity 
supply.
